# 🔭 Watch Journal — stack evolutions

Journal fed by the watch (`scripts/veille_versions.py` — automatically rerun
at session open if the last run is older than 20 h, via the SessionStart hook).
**Goal: keep AI agents aware** of the local stack's news
(audio.cpp, FFmpeg, Python/packages, GGUF models, llama.cpp) to leverage them
in the project. Every relevant novelty is recorded with its
project impact; pitfalls and validated stacks stay in `MEMORY_BANK.md`.

Format: `YYYY-MM-DD • source • change • project impact / action`

---

- **2026-10-20 (02:2x→02:5x, post-run analysis) • ROUND-3 VALIDATION FAILED (WORKFLOW_EXIT=1, `vk::Queue::submit: ErrorOutOfDeviceMemory` at step 2 segment 8/54) — root cause identified: WATERMARK OVER-RECLAMATION, not the gate itself; fix refined (demotion now shortfall-only), revalidation `h3_CR_ROUND3B_975` IN FLIGHT** — ① The run completed step 1 (54 segments) then crashed in step 2 at segment 8 with the #1976 signature while all reported dimensions were healthy (device 13.5 GB free, host 6.4 GB availPhys). ② Forensics: end of step 1, availPhys dipped to ~977 MB (< 1 GiB watermark) during a MAIN ensure check → `host_reclaim_needed` → demote loop released an **11.36 GB / 551-tensor CPU params block to Disk** (available host 465 MB → 11.2 GB after) to fix a ~30 MB transient shortfall → the reload churn (11 GB re-read + re-pin + re-stage while the pipeline was live) preceded the submit OOM 8 segments later. Prefetch was healthy (59 activations ≈ 1/segment executed, 0 miss/0 fail — the 413-vs-59 gap was run length, not regression). ③ Run 6 ALSO dipped below the watermark (available host 99.86 MB ≈ availPhys 612 MB!) but its dip coincided with a PREFETCH capacity check (silent skip, no churn) — the old code had the same over-reclamation property and passed twice by timing luck; tonight's dip hit a main check. ④ **Refinement implemented**: `host_short` now = `required_host_bytes > available_host_bytes` ONLY (watermark removed from demotion eligibility) — transient dips no longer evict whole blocks; the watermark still gates prefetch and the final acceptance (refusal = clean failure, strictly better than crash). This vindicates CR's instinct on the watermark coupling in a way our two completions had masked. ⑤ Zombie sd-cli (9.5 GB RAM, leftover of the crashed run) killed before relaunch; gate green again. Round-3 fixes still LOCAL (no commit/push until the rig passes). If ROUND3B completes → commit+push+PR body; if it refuses cleanly at a dip → honest policy outcome, discuss; if it crashes again → hold everything.

- **2026-09-11 • sd-cli • BREAKthrough #1946: leejet requests 2 diag runs (`GGML_VK_MEMORY_LOGGER=1` ± `GGML_VK_DISABLE_HOST_VISIBLE_VIDMEM=1`) — the 2nd SUCCEEDS: `--offload-to-cpu` works, correct image, and faster; cause = the AMD driver's 256 MB host-visible VRAM window** — leejet's request (17:49 UTC, [comment 5638526154](https://github.com/leejet/stable-diffusion.cpp/issues/1946#issuecomment-5638526154)), after reading our debug log: failure = first execution of the T5 graph after OK staging; he found a cleanup path that could raise a 2nd exception during failure handling (hence the `terminate` reporting `Queue::submit` instead of the real `createComputePipeline` — the new #1958 handler never gets its chance); he suspects the interaction between the refactor's small allocations and Vulkan's preference for CPU-visible VRAM. **Runs executed (same build `58d3f6e`, same apple command seed 42, free machine verified)**: ① run 1 = usual crash (exit 127) with the memory logger — T5 staging 9,083.77 MB OK in 5.84 s, 68.25 MB compute buffer allocated, then `createComputePipeline: ErrorOutOfDeviceMemory` on `rms_norm_mul_f32` with "Total device: 9.17 GiB" and ~6 GB free; ② **run 2 = exit 0, `out_memlogger_nohv.png` generated (conforming apple, seed 42)** — same T5 staging in 1.25 s (vs 5.84 s), conditioning 4.71 s, sampling 11.65 s (4 steps), VAE 1.41 s: **not only does it work, everything is faster without host-visible VRAM**. `vulkaninfo` (heaps/types section archived `scratch/bisect_1946/vulkaninfo_memory_section.txt`) explains the squeezing: **heap 2 = only 256 MB** (`DEVICE_LOCAL|HOST_VISIBLE`, type 2) on this card — heap 0 = 15.73 GiB pure DEVICE_LOCAL, heap 1 = 15.92 GiB shared/GTT; with the host-visible preference active, device allocations OOM against that tiny window despite the nearly empty heap 0; deactivated, everything goes device-local. **Root cause of the `--offload-to-cpu` crash identified and workaround validated locally** (env var, no code fix yet); the question of the "unified graph" OOM without offload (segment 30/59) remains open but leejet now has all the elements. EN answer draft ready: `scratch/bisect_1946/commentaire_memlogger_BROUILLON.md` (logs of the 2 runs: `leejet_memlogger.log` 531 l., `leejet_memlogger_nohostvisible.log` 1787 l.; gists to create at publication). **Action: publication of the 2 logs + vulkaninfo + result = published the same day with user agreement: [comment #issuecomment-5638768931](https://github.com/leejet/stable-diffusion.cpp/issues/1946#issuecomment-5638768931) + [gist of 3 files (2 full logs + vulkaninfo)](https://gist.github.com/laurentvv/6422321845c3466179f20f7ec5c0cf80); to suggest to leejet: gate host-visible by heap size or default off if a small device-local host-visible heap.**

- **2026-09-06 • ace-step/audio.cpp • audio-conditioned routes (cover/repaint/lego/extract) blocked on Vulkan**: the VAE encoder requires a single ~4.5-4.8 GiB buffer, above the AMD Windows driver's `maxBufferSize` limit (4 GB) — a **per-buffer** limit (VK_KHR_maintenance4), not total VRAM (hence no visible VRAM peak). Fixed-size buffer, independent of the source's duration. **Validated workaround: `--backend cpu --threads 20`** (30 s cover in 77 s, RTF 2.58, 2B turbo). Real test: cover of "Love Like Blood" (German band) reinterpreted as Vent-Gris dark folk. **Project impact: reinterpretations anchored on existing audio = CPU mandatory for now**; if a future audio.cpp/ggml release splits this buffer or the AMD driver raises the limit → retest on Vulkan.
